Each scans 4 theme/contrast states at 1280 px plus dark-standard at 360 px with axe-core 4.12.1 and no rule filtering, asserting zero serious/critical violations and zero unmocked API calls.
- e2e/visual-quality.spec.js (1 test) — /, /login and /chat at 360/768/1280/1536 px: exactly one h1, horizontal overflow ≤1 px, no duplicate element IDs, no visible interactive control without an accessible name, no visible img without alt, and zero console errors across the whole matrix.

### Gotchas

- **`e2e/global-setup.js` is dead code.** Playwright's `createGlobalSetupTasks` runs `createRemoveOutputDirsTask()` *before* the config's globalSetups (node_modules/playwright/lib/runner/index.js:5852-5858, 5943-5963), and `preserveOutputDir` is not set, so the whole `test-results/` tree — axe-shards included — is already removed before global-setup.js executes. The rm is harmless but never does anything.
- **The coverage gate covers 21 files, none of them React.** Node's V8 coverage only reports modules actually loaded, and the unit tests import only pure helpers. The lcov.info uploaded to SonarQube contains exactly 21 `SF:` entries (sceneCapability, 3 design modules, 8 lib modules, archiveFilters, authUtils, 3 chat modules, novelty/report, 3 upload modules). `src/api.js`, `main.jsx`, `AuthContext.jsx`, `App.jsx`, every `.jsx` component and every page are outside the 85/85/80 gate entirely. The README's claim that adding lcov fixed a 36.3% whole-repo figure is true only for those 21 files.
- **visual-quality.spec.js is not fail-closed** while the other two specs are. Its route handler (line 46) fulfills every unmatched `/__e2e_api` path with `200 []`, so a page silently getting an empty array instead of a 501 will pass. The README's blanket "an unexpected request fails instead of reaching Supabase, storage, the backend, or Gemini" does not hold for this spec.
- **The 501 guard covers `/__e2e_api` only, not `/__e2e_supabase`.** vite.config.js installs no middleware for the Supabase origin, so an unmocked Supabase call in e2e falls through to the SPA history fallback and gets `200 text/html` — exactly the confusing failure mode the `/__e2e_api` guard exists to prevent. accessibility.spec.js:301-304 works around it by blanket-mocking `**/__e2e_supabase/**`; critical-flows mocks it only in the two auth tests that need it.
- **Three critical-flows tests install no `page.route` at all** (the theme test at :112, the password-reset test at :157, the captcha test at :192), so their real API traffic hits the preview-server 501 guard unobserved and unasserted. The theme test navigates to `/` where Landing fetches `/analytics/summary`; nothing checks the resulting error state.
- **`keyFor` (critical-flows.spec.js:34-37) discards the query string**, so `GET /papers?year=2026` and `GET /papers` collide on one handler key. It also `replace`s only the first `/__e2e_api` occurrence.
- **The `{__status,__body}` envelope is ambiguous for falsy bodies**: `route.fulfill({json: response?.__body ?? response})` means a handler returning `null`/`0` fulfills with that value, and a real API body that happens to carry a `__status` field would be reinterpreted as an envelope.
- **`unexpected`/`unmocked` are asserted synchronously at test end.** A request that arrives after the last assertion (e.g. a trailing poll) is recorded but never checked.
- **The playwright.config.js parallelism comment is stale.** It reasons about "the 24-test suite" and 180 s/126 s/134 s at 1/2/3 workers; `playwright test --list` reports **26 tests in 3 files** today (13 critical-flows + 12 accessibility + 1 visual-quality).
- **`scripts/*.mjs` and `e2e/*.mjs` are effectively unlinted.** eslint.config.js's only rule block is `files: ['**/*.{js,jsx}']`, which does not match `.mjs`; ESLint 9 still *reads* those files but no config object matches, so `js.configs.recommended` (and therefore `no-undef`) never applies. That is why `check-bundle-budget.mjs` and `vite-server.mjs` can use bare `process.*` while every `.js`/`.jsx` spec has to write `import process from 'node:process'` / `import { Buffer } from 'node:buffer'` purely to satisfy `globals.browser`.
- **`check-bundle-budget.mjs` throws rather than reports** if `dist/assets/` does not exist (`readdirSync` at :128 is unguarded, unlike the `index.html` existence check at :76). It also reads `dist/assets` non-recursively, so nested emitted chunks would be unmeasured.
- **`MUST_STAY_LAZY` is a name check, not a reachability check.** If a static import caused rolldown to *merge* useSceneRuntime's code into the entry or into `vendor`, the prefix `useSceneRuntime` would never appear in the eager set and only the 330 kB total would catch it. With 5.3 kB of headroom today that backstop happens to be effective, but the named check alone is necessary-not-sufficient — the opposite of what the file header implies.
- **The eager budget has 5.3 kB of headroom** (324.7 / 330). Any new statically-imported dependency of the entry graph, or a Tailwind CSS growth in `index-*.css` (24.3 kB today), fails the gate.
- **`E2E_AUTH_STORAGE_KEY` is exported from e2eSession.js:21 and imported by nothing.** The literal `'isu_e2e_auth_fixture'` is re-declared in critical-flows.spec.js:4 and accessibility.spec.js:26. Renaming the key in one place silently breaks the fixtures with no test failure to point at it.
- **`docs/evidence/accessibility/` does not exist in the tree**, so `CAPTURE_A11Y_EVIDENCE=1` has never been committed from — the a11y gate runs in CI but leaves no dated evidence bundle, unlike visual-baselines which has its 8 PNGs.
- **`VITE_SUPABASE_URL` in e2e is a hard-coded absolute `http://127.0.0.1:4173/...`.** Changing the preview port in playwright.config.js/vite-server.mjs without changing vite.config.js:86 turns every Supabase call cross-origin.
- **critical-flows.spec.js:112 reimplements WCAG contrast math locally** (parseHex/composite/relativeLuminance/contrastRatio at :64-89) instead of importing `src/design/contrast.js`, which the unit suite already pins against the WCAG reference values — two independent implementations of the same formula.
- **`expectReadableCard` asserts `--card` is 8-digit hex and `--surface-0..3` are 6-digit hex**, which is only true because `src/design/materialTheme.js:47,73-76` overwrites the `rgba()` values declared statically in `src/index.css:188,214-217`. Reverting to the CSS defaults would fail the regex, not the contrast math. The test also carries a bare magic-value regression guard, `expect(lightTokens.card).not.toBe('#000000E6')`.
- **Seven unit test files assert on source text rather than behaviour** — badgeTones (scans two admin .jsx files for `<Badge tone=…>`), fontDelivery (index.html + nginx template), materialTheme (greps its own implementation for `neutral.tone(tone)` and literal `highContrast ? 'FF' : '38'`), idleSession (three importer files), privilegedMfa (Admin.jsx's gate map), messageNotice / responseActions / transcript (Chat.jsx, sliced by `const <name> = ` boundaries and matched with regexes like `/send\(updated, \{\s*baseMessages: branch\.messages,/`). They are brittle to pure refactors — renaming a `const` in Chat.jsx or reformatting the gate object fails tests that have nothing to do with the change.
- **`src/design/fontDelivery.test.js` runs file reads and an `assert.ok(csp)` at module top level**, outside any `test()`. If the nginx template loses its CSP the failure is a module-load error, not a named test failure.
- **`src/pages/archive/archiveCatalog.test.js` tests `./archiveFilters.js`** — the only file whose test name does not match its subject.
- **`npm test` is a bare `node --test` with no `--test-coverage-exclude`**, so `npm test` and `npm run test:coverage` discover the same files but only the latter is gated; a developer running `npm test` gets no threshold enforcement.
- **visual-quality's `expect(consoleErrors).toEqual([])` is a single assertion after all 12 iterations**, so the failure message does not say which route/viewport produced the error.
- **`settleAnimations` selects `[style*="opacity"]`**, a substring match on the whole inline style attribute — an element with `style="--foo: opacity"` or a custom property whose name contains `opacity` would be sampled. Its 15 s timeout surfaces as a generic `waitForFunction` failure, not as "animations never settled".
- **`npm run security:headers` has no CI wiring and no test.** The CSP it regex-checks is also checked, differently and statically, by `src/design/fontDelivery.test.js` against nginx/default.conf.template — two overlapping validators with no shared source of truth.
- **Node's default test glob is load-bearing and undeclared.** Nothing in the repo pins that `node --test` skips `e2e/*.spec.js`; a future Node release that widens the default glob to `*.spec.js` would make `npm test` try to execute Playwright specs outside a Playwright runner.

### Open questions

- Should visual-quality.spec.js be made fail-closed like the other two specs? Its `responses[pathname] ?? []` currently masks any endpoint the public surfaces start calling.
- Is `/__e2e_supabase` intended to be unguarded? Adding a mirror of the `/__e2e_api` middleware would make the README's fail-closed claim literally true and turn silent index.html responses into 501s.
- `e2e/global-setup.js` can be deleted (Playwright already clears test-results before globalSetup) — or kept only if `preserveOutputDir` is ever adopted. It is currently the only reason `globalSetup` is configured at all.
- The 85/85/80 coverage gate applies to 21 helper modules and no React code. Is that the intended claim in the paper's Functional Suitability row, or should the gate be re-scoped (e.g. an explicit include list, or a DOM-capable renderer) so the number means what a reader would assume?
- The eager payload is at 324.7 / 330 kB. Was 330 chosen as headroom over a measured baseline, or is the budget effectively already at the ceiling?
- docs/evidence/accessibility/ has never been populated even though the a11y matrix runs in CI — should the workflow set CAPTURE_A11Y_EVIDENCE=1 and upload the report as a dated artifact the way backend coverage is uploaded?
- The playwright.config.js worker-count comment cites a 24-test measurement; the suite is now 26 tests. Re-measure or drop the specific timings.
- `npm run security:headers` is documented and implemented but unreachable from any pipeline — is it meant to be a post-deploy step someone runs by hand, and is that written down anywhere?

**Depends on:** @playwright/test 1.61.1 and @axe-core/playwright 4.12.1 (the version strings are also hard-coded into the axe-report.json metadata at accessibility.spec.js:541-542 and will drift silently from package.json), vite 8.1.5 — the harness uses its programmatic build()/preview() API and depends on userDefine winning over env-derived import.meta.env keys, Node 24.18.0's built-in test runner, coverage and lcov reporter; node:zlib gzipSync for the budget, src/testing/e2eSession.js, consumed by api.js, AuthContext.jsx, main.jsx, Admin.jsx, IdleSessionGuard.jsx and components/security/turnstileConfig.js, src/design/contrast.js and src/design/tokens.js (the tokens module is deliberately free of @material/material-color-utilities so bare Node ESM can import it), src/index.css, index.html and nginx/default.conf.template — read as data by badgeTones.test.js and fontDelivery.test.js, src/pages/Chat.jsx, src/pages/Admin.jsx, src/pages/admin/SystemManagementTab.jsx, src/pages/admin/OperationsTab.jsx, src/api.js, src/components/IdleSessionGuard.jsx, src/pages/Login.jsx — read as source text by the seven grep-style unit tests, rag-thesis-backend dependencies/auth.py (the PRIVILEGED_MFA_REQUIRED_DETAIL wording) and routers/settings.py DEFAULT_FEATURES (mirrored by src/lib/permissions.js)

**Used by:** .github/workflows/quality.yml `frontend` job — lint, test:coverage, build, bundle:budget, playwright install, test:e2e, in that order; the `sonarqube` job `needs: [backend, frontend]` and consumes the uploaded coverage, sonar-project.properties via sonar.javascript.lcov.reportPaths=rag-thesis-frontend/coverage/lcov.info, rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_readme_accuracy.py (TestFrontendCommandsResolve, TestFrontendReadmeMatchesTheFrontend) — fails the backend suite if the frontend README drifts from package.json or Admin.jsx, docs/evidence/visual-baselines/*.png (8 committed screenshots, produced by CAPTURE_VISUAL_EVIDENCE=1), evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md and the paper's Table 4 (ESLint = Maintainability, the test suites = Functional Suitability, SonarQube = Reliability)

**Additional notes returned by the reader** (fields outside the schema, preserved verbatim)

- `controlFlowAnchors`: n/a

**Adversarial fact-check:** verdict `mostly-accurate`, 13 correction(s), 8 omission(s). Corrections are already folded into the text above. They are kept because each records a wrong belief a competent reader reached from the same files - the same trap is available to you.

- **Wrong:** "Three critical-flows tests install no `page.route` at all (the theme test at :112, the password-reset test at :157, the captcha test at :192), so their real API traffic hits the preview-server 501 guard unobserved."
  **Actual:** False for two of the three. The password-reset test installs `page.route('**/__e2e_supabase/**', ...)` at critical-flows.spec.js:169 (it captures `resetRequest` and asserts the `/auth/v1/recover` payload), and the captcha test installs one at :193 (fulfilling 400 `captcha_failed`). Only the theme test at :112 installs no route at all. The defensible version of the gotcha is narrower: three tests install no `/__e2e_api` mock and therefore never build or assert an `unexpected` array — `mockApi` is called in exactly 10 of the 13 tests (grep for `mockApi(` → :134, :144, :212, :269, :300, :328, :395, :447, :525, :653). (`rag-thesis-frontend/e2e/critical-flows.spec.js:169, :193, :112`)
- **Wrong:** "The theme test navigates to `/` where Landing fetches `/analytics/summary`; nothing checks the resulting error state."
  **Actual:** Understated — the landing page fires two unmocked calls, not one. `Landing` renders `StatsStrip` (src/pages/landing/StatsStrip.jsx:3,12 → `getPublicSummary` → `api.get('/analytics/summary')` at src/api.js:345) *and* `TracksMarquee` (src/pages/landing/TracksMarquee.jsx:29 → `getTracks` → `/upload/tracks`). Both 501 unobserved in that test. The visual-quality spec's own fixture table mocks exactly those two paths (visual-quality.spec.js:35,40), which corroborates the pair. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/Landing.jsx:1-3, src/pages/landing/StatsStrip.jsx:12, src/pages/landing/TracksMarquee.jsx:29`)
- **Wrong:** test_readme_accuracy.py "Pins four frontend README facts from Python ... (lines 147-195)", and the invariant "`test:coverage` and `bundle:budget` must both remain in package.json scripts AND be named in rag-thesis-frontend/README.md (test_readme_accuracy.py:190-195)".
  **Actual:** The 147-195 range straddles two classes with two different targets, and the map collapses them. `TestFrontendCommandsResolve` (147-155) does NOT read the frontend README: it calls `readme_text()`, defined at :24-25 as `README.read_text()` where `README = ROOT / 'README.md'` (:20) — the repo-root README. It parametrizes over FOUR scripts (`lint`, `test:coverage`, `build`, `bundle:budget`, :148) and requires each to exist in package.json AND the literal string `npm run <script>` to appear in the ROOT README — satisfied only by the single chained line README.md:293 (`cd ../rag-thesis-frontend && npm run lint && npm run test:coverage && npm run build && npm run bundle:budget`). Deleting or rewording that one root-README line fails four backend tests. The map's invariant list omits this constraint entirely. Only `TestFrontendReadmeMatchesTheFrontend` (158-195) reads the frontend README. (`rag-thesis-backend/tests/test_readme_accuracy.py:20-25, :147-155; README.md:293`)
- **Wrong:** "Three independent gate layers guard the React client ... A fourth, non-CI script validates production security headers. All of the first three run in `.github/workflows/quality.yml`." / usedBy lists only the `frontend` job.
  **Actual:** Misses a fourth CI gate on the frontend: the separate `frontend-audit` job at .github/workflows/quality.yml:83-139, which runs `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high --fetch-timeout=300000 --fetch-retries=0` inside a 3-attempt backoff loop (15/30/0 s). It is deliberately split out of the `frontend` job — the inline comment at :93-98 records that when npm's advisory endpoint was unreachable on 2026-09-04 it took ESLint, the unit tests, the build, the bundle budget and Playwright down with it and discarded the caches. A high/critical advisory fails on the first attempt; an endpoint that never answers also fails rather than passing an unverified tree. The frontend image is additionally Trivy-scanned and SBOM'd in the `image-scan` matrix (:231-282). (`.github/workflows/quality.yml:83-139, :231-282`)
- **Wrong:** "eslint.config.js's only rule block is `files: ['**/*.{js,jsx}']`, which does not match `.mjs`."
  **Actual:** There are two config objects, not one: the main block at 13-53 and a second at 54-60 scoped to `['**/context/**/*.jsx', '**/components/ui/Motion.jsx', '**/pages/auth/AuthFx.jsx']` that turns off `react-refresh/only-export-components`. The conclusion still holds — neither matches `.mjs`, confirmed empirically: `npx eslint --print-config scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs` returns `"rules": {}`, and `npx eslint scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs e2e/vite-server.mjs --no-warn-ignored` exits 0 with no output despite both files using bare `process.*`. (`rag-thesis-frontend/eslint.config.js:13-53, :54-60`)
- **Wrong:** openQuestion: "`npm run security:headers` is documented and implemented but unreachable from any pipeline — is it meant to be a post-deploy step someone runs by hand, and is that written down anywhere?"
  **Actual:** It is written down, in two places the map did not search: docs/OPERATIONS_SECURITY_RUNBOOK.md:95 and rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md:1214, both giving the exact `-- https://your-deployment.example` invocation. It is absent from both READMEs (neither `rag-thesis-frontend/README.md` nor the root README mentions it), which is why it escapes `test_readme_accuracy.py`'s script-documentation check — that test only parametrizes over lint/test:coverage/build/bundle:budget. (`docs/OPERATIONS_SECURITY_RUNBOOK.md:95, rag-thesis-backend/evaluation/iso25010_evidence.md:1214`)
- **Wrong:** sceneCapability.test.js "Hard blocks that `effects:'full'` cannot override (NO_WEBGL ..., REDUCED_MOTION, SAVE_DATA, LOW_MEMORY <4 GB, LOW_CPU <4 cores); soft blocks it can (COARSE_POINTER, SMALL_VIEWPORT); ... PAGE_HIDDEN ...; an exhaustiveness test proving every BLOCK_REASONS value is reachable."
  **Actual:** The enumeration is 8 of 9 reasons — `LOW_EFFECTS` is missing. `BLOCK_REASONS` has nine members (sceneCapability.js:35-44: NO_WEBGL, REDUCED_MOTION, LOW_EFFECTS, SAVE_DATA, LOW_MEMORY, LOW_CPU, COARSE_POINTER, SMALL_VIEWPORT, PAGE_HIDDEN) and the exhaustiveness test probes all nine (:141-150). LOW_EFFECTS sits inside the same "hard constraints" describe but is asserted outside the parametrized loop (:61-65), with a comment at :53-55 explaining why: its signal *is* the `effects` field, so setting `effects:'full'` replaces the condition rather than testing override resistance. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/components/three/sceneCapability.js:35-44; src/components/three/sceneCapability.test.js:53-65, :141-150`)
- **Wrong:** authUtils.test.js covers "both captcha variants — 'security check expired or failed' vs 'security is misconfigured' — the exact pair critical-flows exercises end to end."
  **Actual:** Only one of the pair is exercised end to end. critical-flows.spec.js:192-208 stubs Supabase with `msg: 'captcha verification process failed'` and asserts `/Authentication security is misconfigured/` — the second variant only. The first variant ('Captcha verification failed' → `/security check expired or failed/i`, authUtils.test.js:35) has no e2e counterpart; the other captcha-adjacent e2e test (:157) exercises a *successful* Turnstile solve, asserting `gotrue_meta_security.captcha_token`, not a failure message. (`rag-thesis-frontend/e2e/critical-flows.spec.js:192-208, :157-190; src/pages/auth/authUtils.test.js:35-36`)
- **Wrong:** Bundle-budget errorHandling lists only: budget exceeded, zero-eager-asset parse failure, missing index.html, and the unguarded `readdirSync` throw on missing dist/assets.
  **Actual:** Misses a fourth, coded failure mode: an eager href present in index.html but absent from disk is pushed as the violation `index.html preloads <href>, which is not in dist/` and then skipped from the byte sum (check-bundle-budget.mjs:96-99). This matters because it is the one case where the eager total is computed over a *subset* of the declared eager set — the gate reports the shortfall rather than silently under-measuring, which is the same design intent as the zero-href guard the map does cite. (`rag-thesis-frontend/scripts/check-bundle-budget.mjs:96-99`)
- **Wrong:** "`expectReadableCard` asserts `--card` is 8-digit hex and `--surface-0..3` are 6-digit hex" (plus the contrast check and the `#000000E6` guard).
  **Actual:** Incomplete: it also asserts the four surface tokens are mutually distinct — `expect(new Set(tokens.surfaces).size).toBe(4)` at :108. That is a separate regression guard (a theme collapsing surface-0..3 onto one value would pass both the hex regexes and the contrast check). (`rag-thesis-frontend/e2e/critical-flows.spec.js:105-110`)
- **Wrong:** visual-quality.spec.js "1 test: structural DOM audit of /, /login, /chat at 360/768/1280/1536 px; optional screenshot evidence capture" — its mocking is described only as the non-fail-closed `/__e2e_api` handler.
  **Actual:** `mockPublicApi` stubs three origins, not one: `https://fonts.googleapis.com/**` → empty CSS (:15-19), `https://challenges.cloudflare.com/**` → a fake `window.turnstile` whose `render` auto-invokes `options.callback('e2e-turnstile-token')` (:20-31), and only then the `/__e2e_api` handler (:32-47). The Cloudflare stub matters: without it /login's Turnstile widget would reach the real challenge origin during the audit. (`rag-thesis-frontend/e2e/visual-quality.spec.js:14-48`)
- **Wrong:** keyFiles describes playwright.config.js as "testDir ./e2e, globalSetup, fullyParallel:false, workers CI?2:1, retries CI?2:0, forbidOnly on CI, baseURL 127.0.0.1:4173, single chromium project, webServer ...".
  **Actual:** Omits `outputDir: 'test-results'` (:18), which is the load-bearing fact in the map's own global-setup dead-code argument — `createRemoveOutputDirsTask` deletes `p.project.outputDir` (node_modules/playwright/lib/runner/index.js:5943-5963), and `test-results/axe-shards/` is inside it, which is *why* the rm in global-setup.js is redundant. Also omits `gracefulShutdown: { signal: 'SIGINT', timeout: 2_000 }` (:30), the mechanism by which Playwright's own webServer teardown reaches vite-server.mjs's `process.once('SIGINT', close)`. (`rag-thesis-frontend/playwright.config.js:18, :30`)
- **Wrong:** transcript.test.js — "three source scans slice Chat.jsx by `const <name> = ` boundaries".
  **Actual:** Four tests read Chat.jsx, of which three slice by `const <name> = ` boundaries (:63-74 updatePrompt, :76-98 stopWaiting+updatePrompt, :100-119 loadSession) and a fourth greps the whole file un-sliced for `message.notice_type === 'conversation'`, `.map((message) => message.answer)` and `.slice(-30)` (:121-126). Minor, but it changes the brittleness surface the map is describing: the fourth scan breaks on any reformatting anywhere in Chat.jsx, not just inside one const's body. (`rag-thesis-frontend/src/pages/chat/transcript.test.js:63-126`)

Omissions the checker caught:

- A fourth frontend CI gate exists and is omitted entirely: the `frontend-audit` job (.github/workflows/quality.yml:83-139) runs `npm audit --omit=dev --audit-level=high` with a 3-attempt retry loop, split out of the `frontend` job on purpose (comment at :93-98). The frontend container is also Trivy-scanned and SBOM'd in the `image-scan` matrix (:231-282, context `rag-thesis-frontend`), including a smoke test of the built image.
- The root README is a pinned surface for the frontend scripts. test_readme_accuracy.py:147-155 requires the literal `npm run lint`, `npm run test:coverage`, `npm run build` and `npm run bundle:budget` to appear in ROOT README.md — currently satisfied only by the single chained line at README.md:293. The map's invariants cover only the frontend README.
- `src/testing/` contains exactly one file, e2eSession.js (708 bytes). The map's framing ("src/testing and e2e: fail-closed local fixtures" per the frontend README:12) can read as though a fixture directory exists; there is no fixture data module, every fixture body is inline in the specs.
- `main.jsx` carries a second, unrelated interceptor-coupling invariant the map does not mention: `INTERCEPTOR_RETRIED_STATUSES = new Set([502, 504])` (main.jsx:31) must stay in step with `TRANSIENT_GATEWAY_STATUSES` in src/api.js:17, with the comment at main.jsx:28-30 recording that duplicating the retry turned one failed GET into six requests. Nothing tests this pairing — it is an untested cross-file constant duplication in the same subsystem the map audits.
- The e2e Supabase client is constructed for real and will throw at module load if either literal is empty: src/supabaseClient.js:6-12 hard-throws 'Missing Supabase config'. In e2e mode the two `define` literals (vite.config.js:86-87) are what keep that throw from firing — so the define override is not merely a leak guard, it is load-bearing for the app booting at all under `--mode e2e`.
- `node --test` discovery was verified empirically rather than assumed: 209 tests / 7 suites / 0 fail in ~490 ms, and the three `e2e/*.spec.js` files are not picked up. The 7 suites are all in sceneCapability.test.js (3 top-level `describe` + 4 nested), which is the only file using `describe`/`it`; the other 21 files use bare `test()`.
- `chunkPrefix()` (check-bundle-budget.mjs:57-60) strips `-<hash of 6+ chars>.(js|css)` — so the `MUST_STAY_LAZY` name check silently depends on rolldown continuing to emit hashes of length >= 6 AND on the chunk name surviving as the filename stem. A build config change to `entryFileNames`/`chunkFileNames` (e.g. a flat hash-only name) would make every prefix unrecognizable, the `MUST_STAY_LAZY` check vacuous, and every chunk fall through to the 150 kB default cap — under which `useSceneRuntime` (232.08 kB) and `OverviewCharts` (107.08 kB) would produce one spurious violation and one spurious pass respectively. Neither the zero-href guard nor any test covers this.
- `api.js`'s dev-only readiness wait is disabled in e2e by construction, not by the `isE2ETestMode` branch: `SHOULD_WAIT_FOR_DEV_BACKEND = import.meta.env.DEV && !import.meta.env.VITE_API_URL` (api.js:20) is false in an e2e build both because DEV is false and because VITE_API_URL is defined to '/__e2e_api'. This is the reason the absent `/__backend-ready` preview middleware never matters — worth stating as the mechanism, since the map asserts the outcome without it.
